- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Askia, leader of Songhai (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Songhai
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Mandekalu Cavalry, Replacing Knight (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Unique Unit: Sofa, Replacing Crossbowman (Requires technology: Machinery)
        - Unique Building: Tabya, Replacing Stone Works (Requires technology: Construction)
        - Unique Building: Gumey, Replacing Caravansary (Requires technology: Currency)
        - Civilization Ability: Triple Gold from destroying Encampments and plundering Cities. Land Units gain the Amphibious and War Canoes Promotions.
      - Leader: Askia
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Radar: Allows you to build the Heavy Bomber, a deadly air Unit capable of delivering death from afar, and the Fighter, an air Unit designed to wrest control of the skies from enemy aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Advanced Ballistics, Mobile Tactics
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the game (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      
      ## Policies
      - Dictatorship of the Proletariat (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Dictatorship of the Proletariat: +7 Food, Science, Gold, and Culture per city.
      - Academy of Sciences (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Academy of Sciences: -2 Unhappiness from Illiteracy in all Cities, +100% Production towards Research Labs, and receive 5 Free Research Labs in your Empire. Research Labs generate an additional +2 Science.
      - Five-Year Plan (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Five-Year Plan: +20% Production towards Buildings in all Cities. +3 Production for every Mine, Quarry, Lumber Mill, Oil Well, and Unique Improvement.
      - Resettlement (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Resettlement: New Cities have +3 Population. All Cities gain +2 Citizens immediately. No Partisans from razing enemy Cities.
      - Martial Law (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * No Gold Maintenance from Garrisons.
        - * +1 Happiness and +4 Culture for each Garrison.
        - * -20% Yield penalties of Puppeted Cities.
      - Nationalization (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Nationalization: Each Corporate Office counts as a Franchise. Foreign Franchises no longer benefit your Corporation, you can no longer construct Franchises in foreign Cities (except Vassals), and foreign Corporations can no longer set up Franchises in your Cities (except Masters). +2 Science from all Corporation Offices.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Cultural Revolution (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Cultural Revolution: +25% Tourism to civilizations with less Happiness. +1 Happiness from Factories.
      - Great Leap Forward (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Great Leap Forward: Receive a free Technology. Spies gain +100% Science. +1 Science in Cities for every 3 non-Specialist Citizens.
      - Workers' Faculties (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Worker Faculties: Factories increase City Science output by 10%. +100% Production towards Factories, and receive 5 Free Factories in your Empire.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - Free Thought (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest in all Cities.
        - * +25% Instant Yields from Great Scientists.
        - * Earn Great Scientists +33% faster.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Progress (New Branch):
        - Progress is best for civilizations which desire robust infrastructure and scientific advancement.
        - Adopting Progress grants:
        - * Receive 20 Science when a Citizen is born in your Capital and 15 Science for every Citizen already in your Capital, both scaling with Era.
        - * Receive 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era, and for every Technology already unlocked (not scaling with Era).
        - Each Progress policy unlocked grants:
        - * Gain an additional 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era.
        - Adopting all policies in Progress grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Forbidden Palace.
        - * Receive 25 Gold when a Citizen is born in any city, scaling with Era.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Writers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 5
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 10
        - Boldness: 10

        - HostileBias: 10

        - DenounceWillingness: 10
        - Forgiveness: 1
        - Meanness: 10
        - Neediness: 1
        - Chattiness: 1
        - DeceptiveBias: 5
        - Rationale: We are focusing on maintaining city-state alliances for resources and World Congress votes, not hostility. Lowering hostile bias ensures our AI does not unnecessarily antagonize city-states while we pursue domination.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Radar
        - Rationale: Radar unlocks Heavy Bombers and Fighters, providing critical air superiority and bombing capability to complement our upcoming nuclear arsenal. This will allow us to shatter Chinese defenses and achieve air dominance in the upcoming war.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Industry (New Branch)
        - Rationale: Industry's economic bonuses will strengthen our war economy, enabling faster unit purchases and infrastructure development.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Jerusalem
        - Public: -30
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: Jerusalem is at peace with us but remains Japan's ally and could redeclare. Keeping moderate hostility maintains caution without forcing immediate war, allowing us to focus on China and Japan. No change needed.
        - UpdatedTurn: 146
        
        ### Bunkeya
        - Public: -30
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: Jerusalem is at peace with us but remains Japan's ally and could redeclare. Keeping moderate hostility maintains caution without forcing immediate war, allowing us to focus on China and Japan. No change.
        - UpdatedTurn: 242
        
        ### The Aztecs
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: The Aztecs are our current active enemy; we are winning (war score 32%) and capturing their cities (Tzintzuntzen is razing). Maintain maximum hostility to ensure we eliminate them completely before turning to China. No change needed.
        - UpdatedTurn: 344
        
        ### Japan
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Japan is on the brink of collapse (only 3 cities left, war score 26%). We must finish them quickly to free our forces for China and The Aztecs. No peace until they are eliminated.
        - UpdatedTurn: 344
        
        ### Egypt
        - Public: 80
        - Private: 80
        - Rationale: Egypt remains our most critical ally, with a massive military (12530 strength) and shared Order ideology. They are actively helping capture Japanese cities and will be crucial in the upcoming war against China (they have a defensive pact with China, but we expect they will join us when we declare). Maintaining maximum positive relations ensures continued open borders, trade, and military cooperation. We must keep Egypt firmly on our side to avoid a two-front war with both China and Egypt.
        - UpdatedTurn: 357
        
        ### Venice
        - Public: 80
        - Private: 80
        - Rationale: Venice is our defensive pact ally and a valuable trade partner. They provide economic benefits and secure our western flank. Keeping relations at maximum ensures the pact remains active and they do not betray us. Venice is also friendly with China, but our defensive pact with them should prevent them from joining China against us. High relations reduce the risk of them breaking the pact or denouncing us.
        - UpdatedTurn: 357
        
        ### India
        - Public: 60
        - Private: 60
        - Rationale: India is a friendly trade partner and also at war with The Aztecs, providing a useful distraction. While they have ideological differences (Freedom vs. our Order), they are not an immediate threat and can serve as a buffer. We keep relations positive to maintain trade and avoid another enemy, but not so high as to distract from our primary conquest goals. They are also a major diplomatic contender, but our focus is military domination.
        - UpdatedTurn: 357
        
        ### China
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: China is our primary future target; peace treaty expires in 6 turns. They have a defensive pact with The Maya and a strong bomber force. Maximum hostility ensures our AI does not seek peace and continues military buildup. We must be ready to strike immediately after finishing The Aztecs and Japan. China's territory is large (10 cities) and their military is strong (3703 strength), so we need to maintain full war focus.
        - UpdatedTurn: 357
        
        ### The Maya
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: The Maya are in a defensive pact with China and will join any war we start against China. To ensure our AI remains fully committed to fighting them and does not seek peace, we set maximum hostility. They are currently unhappy and at war with The Aztecs, but they will become a co-belligerent when we attack China. We must be prepared to fight them simultaneously.
        - UpdatedTurn: 358
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: We are on the brink of nuclear warfare and preparing for a massive offensive against China and The Maya. Increasing UseNuke and Nuke flavors ensures aggressive nuclear strike planning and arsenal buildup. Air and Antiair at maximum to dominate the skies and counter China's bomber force. All other military flavors remain high to sustain our multi-front war and rapid conquest.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
        
        ### Flavors

        - Defense: 80
        - Mobilization: 100
        - CityDefense: 90
        - MilitaryTraining: 100
        - Recon: 10

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 43
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 9 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cultural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each World Wonder provides +2 Culture to its city, and each Great Work provides +1 Science to its city.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Indian Sphere of Influence: Dorestad:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 366
          - World Science Initiative:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians.
              Civilizations with Scores from Technologies and Future Tech below the global median will receive a boost to Food and Science up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Egyptian Sphere of Influence: Abernethy:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Religion: Shinto:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Indian Sphere of Influence: Sokoto:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Venetian Sphere of Influence: Kwa-Bulawayo:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 344
          - Indian Sphere of Influence: Riga:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Venetian Sphere of Influence: Oslo:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: The Aztecs:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 355
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Cold War:
            - Description: Ideological differences have pushed the world into a cold war, and economic and political factions are forming based on ideology alone.
              While this resolution is active, players may only make Trade Routes with civilizations that follow the same ideology, or with allied City-States. Furthermore, Influence levels with allied City-States do not decline.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - Proposer: Egypt
          - Enact: Indian Sphere of Influence: La Venta:

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Askia, leader of Songhai (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Songhai
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Mandekalu Cavalry, Replacing Knight (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Unique Unit: Sofa, Replacing Crossbowman (Requires technology: Machinery)
        - Unique Building: Tabya, Replacing Stone Works (Requires technology: Construction)
        - Unique Building: Gumey, Replacing Caravansary (Requires technology: Currency)
        - Civilization Ability: Triple Gold from destroying Encampments and plundering Cities. Land Units gain the Amphibious and War Canoes Promotions.
      - Leader: Askia
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Radar: Allows you to build the Heavy Bomber, a deadly air Unit capable of delivering death from afar, and the Fighter, an air Unit designed to wrest control of the skies from enemy aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Advanced Ballistics, Mobile Tactics
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the game (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      
      ## Policies
      - Dictatorship of the Proletariat (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Dictatorship of the Proletariat: +7 Food, Science, Gold, and Culture per city.
      - Academy of Sciences (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Academy of Sciences: -2 Unhappiness from Illiteracy in all Cities, +100% Production towards Research Labs, and receive 5 Free Research Labs in your Empire. Research Labs generate an additional +2 Science.
      - Five-Year Plan (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Five-Year Plan: +20% Production towards Buildings in all Cities. +3 Production for every Mine, Quarry, Lumber Mill, Oil Well, and Unique Improvement.
      - Resettlement (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Resettlement: New Cities have +3 Population. All Cities gain +2 Citizens immediately. No Partisans from razing enemy Cities.
      - Martial Law (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * No Gold Maintenance from Garrisons.
        - * +1 Happiness and +4 Culture for each Garrison.
        - * -20% Yield penalties of Puppeted Cities.
      - Nationalization (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Nationalization: Each Corporate Office counts as a Franchise. Foreign Franchises no longer benefit your Corporation, you can no longer construct Franchises in foreign Cities (except Vassals), and foreign Corporations can no longer set up Franchises in your Cities (except Masters). +2 Science from all Corporation Offices.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Cultural Revolution (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Cultural Revolution: +25% Tourism to civilizations with less Happiness. +1 Happiness from Factories.
      - Great Leap Forward (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Great Leap Forward: Receive a free Technology. Spies gain +100% Science. +1 Science in Cities for every 3 non-Specialist Citizens.
      - Workers' Faculties (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Worker Faculties: Factories increase City Science output by 10%. +100% Production towards Factories, and receive 5 Free Factories in your Empire.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - Free Thought (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest in all Cities.
        - * +25% Instant Yields from Great Scientists.
        - * Earn Great Scientists +33% faster.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Progress (New Branch):
        - Progress is best for civilizations which desire robust infrastructure and scientific advancement.
        - Adopting Progress grants:
        - * Receive 20 Science when a Citizen is born in your Capital and 15 Science for every Citizen already in your Capital, both scaling with Era.
        - * Receive 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era, and for every Technology already unlocked (not scaling with Era).
        - Each Progress policy unlocked grants:
        - * Gain an additional 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era.
        - Adopting all policies in Progress grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Forbidden Palace.
        - * Receive 25 Gold when a Citizen is born in any city, scaling with Era.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Writers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Analyzing Current Standing**
            
            My focus is on our current standing in Turn 368 of the Atomic/Information Era. I'm assessing our score of 1965, placing us 4th among seven major civilizations, and noting Egypt and India's leading positions.
            
            **Revising Rankings**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our position, now understanding Egypt's dominant cultural lead in Turn 368. Their influence across six civilizations in the Atomic Era, with me at 1965 and 4th, signals an urgent need for strategic adjustments.
            
            **Preventing Impending Victory**
            
            I've identified an immediate threat: Egypt is on the verge of a Cultural Victory by completing the Citizen Earth Protocol. This requires urgent action, potentially even breaking existing alliances, to disrupt their progress and prevent their instant win condition.
            
            **Disrupting Their Ascendancy**
            
            I've confirmed Egypt's imminent Cultural Victory, having achieved influence over all six other civilizations. Their completion of the Citizen Earth Protocol is the final trigger. Despite our alliance and their superior military strength, I must find a way to disrupt their progress.
            
            **Analyzing Alliances**
            
            My current thinking is focused on the ramifications of existing conflicts and alliances. I'm verifying our active war with the Aztecs and the presence of our units in their territory. Simultaneously, I'm reviewing the World Congress agenda and active resolutions, specifically the "Passport System," to assess its impact on Egypt's cultural victory.
            
            **Assessing Strategic Options**
            
            I'm now considering how to disrupt Egypt's impending cultural victory. Since direct military confrontation is unlikely to succeed given their overwhelming strength and heavily fortified capital, I am exploring alternative strategies. This includes examining if there are any diplomatic or technological means available to interfere with the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder itself.
            
            **Researching Disruptive Technologies**
            
            I'm shifting my research focus from Atomic Theory to technologies that can directly counter Egypt's impending Cultural Victory. Nuclear Fission and Computers are now primary candidates, offering potential avenues to interfere with their wonder or global influence, bypassing direct military engagement.
            
            **Reviewing Ideology Choices**
            
            I'm reviewing our current ideological policies within the Order tree. Specifically, I'm examining the available Tier 2 policies like Cultural Revolution and Great Leap Forward, alongside potential new branches and Tier 1 options that could be more effective. This is crucial for adapting our strategy.
            
            **Choosing Ideological Tenets**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our ideology, prioritizing Order tenets like Communism or Workers' Faculties. These seem more beneficial now than pursuing a new Industry branch, especially given our existing investments and the late game stage. This shift aims to bolster our production and potential wonder-building capabilities.
            
            **Forming Counter-Alliances**
            
            I'm now strategizing to counter Egypt's imminent Cultural Victory by leveraging diplomacy. My current focus is on assembling a coalition to challenge Egypt and China. The plan involves securing alliances with India, the Maya, and the Aztecs, then coordinating a simultaneous declaration of war to achieve a balanced military front against our adversaries.
            
            **Assessing Diplomatic Dilemmas**
            
            I'm wrestling with the immediate need to betray Egypt for a win, despite our strong alliances and defensive pact. Their imminent Cultural Victory, evidenced by influencing all six other civs and needing only the Citizen Earth Protocol, outweighs my current diplomatic standing. My research confirms they are on the cusp of completing the wonder, necessitating this difficult choice.
            
            **Provoking a Declaration**
            
            I'm now considering how to manipulate Egypt into declaring war on me, given our existing friendship. Denouncing them or encroaching on their territory are potential triggers to bypass their defensive pact with China and gain Venice's support. This is crucial to disrupt their imminent Cultural Victory.
            
            **Researching Atomic Weapons**
            
            I'm prioritizing Nuclear Fission research over Radar. The ability to build Atomic Bombs could be a game-changer for disrupting Egypt's impending Cultural Victory, especially considering their overwhelming military. I'm now checking our Uranium reserves.
            
            **Researching Fission**
            
            I'm prioritizing Nuclear Fission research over Radar, as Atomic Bombs seem more critical for disrupting Egypt's victory. I've confirmed we lack visible Uranium and will only know after completing Atomic Theory in two turns. If we have it, Nuclear Fission becomes the immediate next step to enable powerful late-game warfare.
            
            **Choosing Ideology Policy**
            
            I'm deliberating between Workers' Faculties and Communism for my next Order policy. Workers' Faculties offers a significant science boost in factory cities, which aligns with my goal of catching up technologically. Communism, however, could drastically reduce my hefty building investment costs and accelerate wonder production.
            
            **Choosing Ideological Tenet**
            
            I've decided to prioritize Workers' Faculties as my next Order policy. The immediate boost to science in factory cities will be crucial for catching up technologically, and I believe this offers a stronger immediate advantage than the production benefits of Communism.
            
            **Analyzing Geographic Spread**
            
            I'm realizing the vast distances between our core territories and captured cities like Tula, Tamuin, and Nagoya. This fragmented empire presents a significant logistical challenge, forcing me to consider multiple fronts, especially with our capital, Gao, so far from our westernmost possessions and nearing Egypt.
            
            **Assessing Immediate Proximity**
            
            My analysis reveals our core territories are in extremely close proximity to Egypt, China, and Venice. The Aztec city of Malinalco is also directly bordering our southwestern possessions, explaining our ongoing conflict. This geographic reality significantly shapes my strategic options moving forward.
            
            **Considering Diplomatic Shifts**
            
            I'm now exploring how altering our diplomatic standing with Egypt might impact their impending victory. Lowering our relationship modifier with them is being considered. I'm also looking at the upcoming World Congress vote, specifically if the "Repeal: Egyptian Sphere of Influence: Abernethy" proposal could indirectly hinder their tourism and delay their Cultural Victory.
            
            **Prioritizing Scientific Advancement**
            
            I'm reassessing our priorities, recognizing the need to balance our strong military focus with advancements in Science and Production. While a defensive posture against Egypt's Cultural Victory is paramount, bolstering our scientific capabilities will be key for long-term viability and potential counter-offensives.
            
            **Prioritizing Victory Conditions**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our current path. While building the International Space Station and United Nations is good for potential Diplomatic Victory, Egypt's imminent Cultural Victory demands immediate attention. I'm now considering how to leverage these projects, or shift to a more military-focused strategy to disrupt their win condition.
            
            **Analyzing Trade Partners**
            
            I'm evaluating our current trade relationships, noting our strong partnerships with Egypt, India, Venice, and China. Maintaining friendly ties with Egypt, despite their impending victory, allows for crucial gold and science income, which I can leverage later.
            
            **Severing Trade Connections**
            
            I'm realizing our shared Shinto religion, open borders, and active trade routes with Egypt are significantly amplifying their tourism modifier towards us. While closing borders and cancelling trade routes could slow future influence, their existing dominance means these actions won't reverse the current status.
            
            **Confirming Victory Conditions**
            
            I've confirmed Egypt has achieved influence with all six other civilizations, meaning they've met the tourism requirement for Cultural Victory. Their imminent win hinges solely on completing the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder and having the necessary ideology tenets. My previous actions to slow their influence are now irrelevant.
            
            **Attacking Thebes**
            
            My current focus is on a direct assault of Egypt's capital, Thebes, to prevent their Cultural Victory. Despite their superior military, Thebes' low defense strength and proximity to Gao offer a viable opportunity. I must capture it before they complete the Citizen Earth Protocol.
            
            **Assessing Military Capabilities**
            
            My focus is on our current military strength and composition for a potential assault. With T-34s, Infantry, Bombers, and various support units, we have a formidable force. However, the obsolescence of unique units like Mandekalu Cavalry and Sofa, and the need to consider China's significant military strength due to defensive pacts, presents a complex challenge.
            
            **Forming War Coalitions**
            
            I'm now analyzing the potential for forming a coalition against Egypt to halt their Cultural Victory. Venice and India are key targets for alliance, and the Maya are already engaged in conflict. While direct AI control over declarations is limited, I'm considering adjusting our relationship with Egypt to a hostile stance to encourage AI-driven war preparations and potential declarations.
            
            **Provoking an Enemy**
            
            I'm now considering a drastic diplomatic maneuver: changing my relationship with Egypt from friendly to actively hostile. This is designed to trigger their AI into initiating conflict, which in turn should activate our defensive pact with Venice, bringing them into the fray. My goal is to orchestrate a situation where Egypt declares war on me first.
            
            **Declaring Hostility**
            
            I'm initiating a shift to a hostile stance with Egypt. While currently at war with the Aztecs and busy near their city, Malinalco, our superior local strength there suggests a swift resolution is possible. This immediate diplomatic change is critical to prompt Egypt's AI into declaring war, thereby engaging Venice through our defensive pact and disrupting their imminent Cultural Victory.
            
            **Shifting Diplomatic Stance**
            
            I'm now pivoting to a hostile relationship with Egypt. This action is intended to provoke their AI into initiating combat, which should then automatically bring our defensive pact ally, Venice, into the conflict. This is a crucial step to disrupt their impending Cultural Victory, especially given our existing war with the Aztecs.
            
            **Researching Disruptive Technologies**
            
            I'm now prioritizing Nuclear Fission research. The potential to develop Atomic Bombs is critical for overwhelming Egypt's massive armies and fortifications at Thebes. This technological path offers the best chance to disrupt their impending Cultural Victory, especially given our current military disadvantage.
            
            **Researching Uranium**
            
            I am now focused on completing Atomic Theory to discover Uranium. If we possess it, I will immediately pursue the Manhattan Project and begin producing Atomic Bombs, believing this offers the best chance to counter Egypt's impending Cultural Victory and disrupt their dominance.
            
            **Refining AI Flavors**
            
            I'm focusing on optimizing AI behaviors. I've confirmed our strong alignment in Production, Gold, Science, Offense, and Espionage. I will increase `Nuke` and `UseNuke` flavors to 100 to ensure atomic weapons are prioritized, and confirm `GrandStrategy` remains set to `Conquest` with no further adjustments needed.
